in the second and fourth case and
Corollary
3.1.10

with Corollary
3.1.11
in the fourth case,
we can derive the following lemma:

Lemma 3.2.6
.

There exists a constant
C(n,\gamma,\Lambda,\theta)
such that for all constant coefficient operators
P
of the form (
3.2.1
) satisfying Assumption (
3.2.2
) with induced boundary operators
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
and all
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0])

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0]}\leq C(n,\gamma,\Lambda,\theta)\big(

\displaystyle[Pu]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0]}^{(0)}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times(-\infty,0]}

\displaystyle+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0(-\infty,0]}\big).

3.3
Localized Schauder Estimates

To localize the results from Lemmas

3.2.1
,

3.2.2
,

3.2.3
,

3.2.4
,

3.2.5
and

3.2.6
,
we require a result due to Simon
[
18
]
. To formulate it, we recall the following definition.

Definition 3.3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a class of sets. We say that a map
S:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
is

(1)

monotone
, if
S(A)\leq S(B)
whenever
A,B\in\mathcal{C}
with
A\subset B
.

(2)

subadditive
, if

S(A)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}S(A_{i})

whenever
A,A_{1},...,A_{N}\in\mathcal{C}
with
A\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}A_{i}
.

Theorem 3.3.2

(Simon’s Absorption Lemma)
.

Let
R>0
,
p_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
,
A\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
be convex, put
\Omega_{\rho}(p):=U_{\rho}(p)\cap A
and let
S
be a nonnegative, monotone and subadditive function on the class
\set{\Omega_{\rho}(p)\ }{\ \rho>0\textrm{ and }p\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}}
. Let
k\in\mathbb{R}
,
\nu\in(0,1]
,
\theta\in(0,1)
and
E>0
. There exists
\delta(n,\theta)>0
and a constant
C=C(n,k,\nu,\theta)
with the following property:

If for all
\Omega_{\rho}(y)\subset\Omega_{R}(p_{0})
with radius
\rho\leq\nu R

\rho^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta\rho}(y))\leq\delta\rho^{k}S(\Omega_{\rho}(y))+E,\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{then}\hskip 14.22636ptR^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta R}(p_{0}))\leq CE.

The set
A
should be thought of as
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
,
R^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0]
etc. so that each
\Omega_{\rho}(p)
is a truncated parabolic ball as in Definition
1.2.1
.

0
0
footnotetext:
Open means open in the relative topology.

Proof.

Let
p\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
r_{2}>r_{1}>0
. We claim that there exists
N(n,\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}})
and points
q_{1},...,q_{N}\in\Omega_{r_{2}}(p)
such that
\Omega_{r_{2}}(p)\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}\Omega_{r_{1}}(q_{i})
.

To see that this is the case, first, note that there are points
\tilde{q}_{1},...,\tilde{q}_{\tilde{N}}\in\bar{U}_{r_{2}}(p)
such that
U_{r_{2}}(p)\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{\tilde{N}}U_{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}(\tilde{q}_{i})
where
\tilde{N}=\tilde{N}(n,\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}})
. For each
1\leq i\leq\tilde{N}
we do the following: If
\tilde{q}_{i}\in A
, we put
q_{i}:=\tilde{q}_{i}
. If
\tilde{q}_{i}\not\in A
but
U_{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}(\tilde{q}_{i})\cap A\neq\emptyset
we select any
q_{i}\in U_{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}(\tilde{q}_{i})\cap A
. Finally, if
U_{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}(\tilde{q}_{i})\cap A=\emptyset
we don’t define a point
q_{i}
. Note that for any
1\leq i\leq\tilde{N}
that falls into either the first or the second case, we have
U_{\frac{r_{1}}{2}}(\tilde{q}_{i})\subset U_{r_{1}}(q_{i})
.

This produces
N\leq\tilde{N}(n,\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}})
and points
q_{1},...,q_{N}\in\Omega_{r_{2}}(p)
such that
\Omega_{r_{2}}(p)\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}\Omega_{r_{1}}(q_{i})
.

Having finished this preparatory consideration, we now start the proof of the theorem. Note that for all
p\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\rho\leq\nu R
we have
S(\Omega_{\rho}(p))\leq S(\Omega_{R}(p_{0}))<\infty
since
S
is monotone and hence

Q:=\sup\set{\rho^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta\rho}(p))\ }{\ \Omega_{\rho}(p)\subset\Omega_{R}(p_{0})\textrm{ and }\rho\leq\nu R}<\infty.

Let
\delta>0
and assume that for all
\rho\leq\nu R
and
\Omega_{\rho}(p)\subset\Omega_{R}(p_{0})
we have

\rho^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta\rho}(p))\leq\delta\rho^{k}S(\Omega_{\rho}(p))+E.

(3.3.1)

Later in the proof, we will derive a suitable condition on the smallness of
\delta
. Given any
q\in\Omega_{R}(p_{0})
and
\rho\in(0,\nu R]
such that
\Omega_{\rho}(q)\subset\Omega_{R}(p_{0})
we can apply Estimate (
3.3.1
) with
\Omega_{\theta\rho}(q)
and obtain

\left(\theta\rho\right)^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta^{2}\rho}(q))\leq\delta\left(\theta\rho\right)^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta\rho}(q))+E=\delta\theta^{k}\rho^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta\rho}(q))+E\leq\delta\theta^{k}Q+E.

The last step is justified by the definition of
Q
. Rearranging, we have proven that for any
\Omega_{\rho}(q)\subset\Omega_{R}(p_{0})
with
\rho\leq\nu R

\rho^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta^{2}\rho}(q))\leq\delta Q+\theta^{-k}E.

(3.3.2)

Now let
p\in\Omega_{R}(p_{0})
and
\rho\in(0,\nu R]
such that
\Omega_{\rho}(p)\subset\Omega_{R}(p_{0})
. By the preparatory consideration, we may cover
\Omega_{\theta\rho}(p)
with balls
\Omega_{\theta^{2}\rho}(q_{i})
where
q_{i}\in\Omega_{\theta\rho}(p)
and
1\leq i\leq N(n,\theta)
. By first using that
S
is subadditive and then applying Estimate (
3.3.2
), we get

\rho^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta\rho}(p))\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\rho^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta^{2}\rho}(q_{i}))\leq N(n,\theta)(\delta Q+\theta^{-k}E).

We now demand that
\delta N(n,\theta)\leq\frac{1}{2}
. This imposes a constraint on
\delta
that only depends on
n
and
\theta
. Taking the supremum over all
\Omega_{\rho}(p)
we get

Q\leq\frac{1}{2}Q+\frac{N(n,\theta)}{\theta^{k}}E\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and hence}\hskip 14.22636ptQ\leq\frac{2N(n,\theta)}{\theta^{k}}E.

(3.3.3)

By the preparatory consideration, there exists
M=M(n,\nu,\theta)\in\mathbb{N}
and
\tilde{q}_{1}
,….,
\tilde{q}_{M}\in\Omega_{\theta R}(p_{0})
such that
\bigcup_{i=1}^{M}\Omega_{\theta\nu R}(\tilde{q}_{i})\supset\Omega_{\theta R}(p_{0})
. Using the subadditivity of
S
and Estimate (
3.3.3
), we get

R^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta R}(p_{0}))\leq\left(\frac{R}{\nu R}\right)^{k}\sum_{i=1}^{M}(\nu R)^{k}S(\Omega_{\theta\nu R}(\tilde{q}_{i}))\leq\nu^{-k}M(n,\nu,\theta)Q\leq C(n,\nu,\theta)E.

∎

3.3.1
Localized Estimates with Constant Coefficients

Throughout this subsection, let
a^{ij}
denote a strictly elliptic matrix and
\theta>0
,
\Lambda>0
such that

|a^{ij}|\leq\Lambda\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636pta^{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\geq\theta|\xi|^{2}\textrm{ for all $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$.}

Additionally, we define the following operators

P:=\partial_{t}+a^{ij}a^{kl}\partial_{ijkl},\hskip 14.22636ptB_{1}:=a^{ni}\partial_{i}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636ptB_{2}:=a^{ni}a^{kl}\partial_{ikl}.

Lemma 3.3.3
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
,
R>0
,
U_{R}:=U_{R}(q)
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R}(q))
. Then

[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}}}^{(0)}\leq C(\Lambda,\theta,n,\gamma)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R}}^{(0)}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R})}\right).

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
\Lambda
,
\theta
,
n
and
\gamma
. Let
p\in U
and
\rho>0
such that
U_{\rho}(p)\subset U_{R}(q)
. Let
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(U_{\rho}(p),[0,1])
with
\eta\equiv 1
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(p)
such that for
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}

\|\partial_{t}^{j}\nabla_{\alpha}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\rho^{-4j-|\alpha|}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636pt[\partial_{t}^{j}\nabla_{\alpha}\eta]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}\leq C\rho^{-4j-|\alpha|-\gamma}.

(3.3.4)

Extending by zero outside of
U_{\rho}(p)
, we define
v:=\eta u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R})
. By Lemma
3.2.1
, we have

[D^{4,1}v]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}\leq C[Pv]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}.

(3.3.5)

To translate this back into an estimate for
u
, we compute

Pv=\eta Pu+\dot{\eta}u+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq 4\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=4\end{subarray}}c(\alpha,\beta)\nabla_{\alpha}u\nabla_{\beta}\eta.

(3.3.6)

For functions
f
and
g
we have the estimate

[fg]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}\leq C\left(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R})}[g]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}+[f]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R})}\right).

(3.3.7)

Using the formula for
Pv
in Equation (
3.3.6
) and Estimate (
3.3.4
), we deduce

\displaystyle[Pv]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p)}^{(0)}+\rho^{-\gamma}\|Pu\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p))}+\rho^{-4}[u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p)}^{(0)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p))}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq 4\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=4\end{subarray}}\rho^{-|\beta|}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p)}^{(0)}+\rho^{-|\beta|-\gamma}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p))}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\leq C\bigg([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p)}^{(0)}+\rho^{-\gamma}\|\dot{u}\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho})}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 4}\rho^{-4+|\alpha|-\gamma}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho})}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\rho^{-4+|\alpha|}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p)}^{(0)}\bigg).

Inserting into Estimate (
3.3.5
), recalling that
v\equiv u
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(p)
and using the interpolation estimates from Theorem
1.2.3
, we obtain

\rho^{4+\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(p)}^{(0)}\leq C(\epsilon)\bigg(\rho^{4+\gamma}[Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p)}^{(0)}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p))}\bigg)+C\epsilon\rho^{4+\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho(p)}}^{(0)}.

(3.3.8)

This estimate is true for all
U_{\rho}(p)\subset U_{R}(q)
. We put
A:=\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
,
\theta_{S}:=\frac{1}{2}
,
\nu:=1
,
k:=4+\gamma
and

E(\epsilon):=C(\epsilon)\bigg(R^{4+\gamma}[Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R}(q)}^{(0)}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R}(q))}\bigg).

Finally, we define
S(U_{\rho}(p)):=[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p)}^{(0)}
. By Proposition
1.1.8
,
S
is subadditive and Estimate (
3.3.8
) becomes

\rho^{k}S(U_{\theta_{S}\rho}(p))\leq\rho^{k}\epsilon S(U_{\rho}(p))+E(\epsilon)\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{for all }U_{\rho}(p)\subset U_{R}(q).

We now fix
\epsilon=\epsilon(n,k):=\frac{1}{2}\delta(n,k)
from Simon’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
and may deduce
R^{k}S(U_{\theta_{S}R}(0))\leq C(n,k,\theta_{S},\nu)E(\epsilon(n,k))
. Recalling the definition of
S
,
E
,
\theta_{S}
and
k
, we obtain

R^{4+\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}^{(0)}\leq C\left(R^{4+\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{R}(q)}^{(0)}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R}(q))}\right),

where
C=C(n,\gamma,\Lambda,\theta)
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.4
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,\infty)
,
R>0
,
U_{R}^{+}:=U_{R}^{+}(q)
,
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R}^{+})
and
u_{0}:=u(\cdot,0)
. Then

[D^{4,1}]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}}^{+}(q)}\leq C(\Lambda,\theta,n,\gamma)\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{R}^{+}(q)}+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R}^{+}(q)}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R}^{+}(q))}\right).

If
\partial_{F}U_{R}^{+}(q)=\emptyset
, the estimate holds with
[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R}^{+}(q)}=0
. Indeed, when
\partial_{F}U_{R}^{+}(q)=\emptyset
, we deduce
U_{R}(q)\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(0,\infty)
and we are in the position where Lemma
3.3.3
applies.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
\Lambda
,
\theta
,
n
and
\gamma
. Let
p\in U_{R}^{+}(q)
and
\rho>0
such that
U_{\rho}^{+}(p)\subset U_{R}^{+}(q)
. We wish to prove that for any
\epsilon>0

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(p)^{+}}\leq

\displaystyle C(\epsilon)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p)}^{(0)}+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{\rho}^{+}(p)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}^{+})}\right)

\displaystyle+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p)}.

(3.3.9)

Once this is established, the lemma follows from Simons’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
with
A=\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,\infty)
by the same arguments that we gave in the proof of Lemma
3.3.3
. To establish Estimate (
3.3.9
), we essentially repeat the argument from Lemma
3.3.3
. We choose a cutoff function
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(U_{\rho}(p),[0,1])
with
\eta\equiv 1
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(p)
that satisfies Estimate (
3.3.4
) and define
v:=\eta u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,\infty))
by extending with
0
outside of
U_{\rho}^{+}(p)
. By Lemma
3.2.2
we have

[D^{4,1}v]^{(0)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,\infty)}\leq C\left([Pv]^{(0)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,\infty)}+[\nabla^{4}v(\cdot,0)]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}}\right).

(3.3.10)

Following the derivation of Estimate (
3.3.8
), we obtain

[Pv]^{(0)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,\infty)}\leq C(\epsilon)\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}^{+}(p))}\right)+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p)}^{(0)}.

(3.3.11)

Next, using the analogue of Estimate (
3.3.7
) for the seminorm
[\cdot]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}}
, the formula

\nabla^{4}(\eta u_{0})=\eta\nabla^{4}u_{0}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq 4\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=4\end{subarray}}c(\alpha,\beta)\nabla_{\beta}\eta\nabla_{\alpha}u_{0}

and Estimate (
3.3.4
), we estimate

\displaystyle[\nabla^{4}(\eta u_{0})]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}}\leq C\bigg(

\displaystyle[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{\rho}^{+}(p)}+\rho^{-\gamma}\|\nabla^{4}u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{F}U_{\rho}^{+}(p))}

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq 4\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=4\end{subarray}}\rho^{-|\beta|}[\nabla_{\alpha}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{\rho}^{+}(p)}+\rho^{-|\beta|-\gamma}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{F}U_{\rho}^{+}(p))}\bigg).

(3.3.12)

Inserting the interpolation estimates from Theorem
1.2.3
, we get

[\nabla^{4}(\eta u_{0})]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}}\leq C(\epsilon)\left([\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{\rho}^{+}(p)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}^{+}(p))}\right)+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p)}^{(0)}.

(3.3.13)

Inserting Estimates (
3.3.11
) and (
3.3.13
) into Estimate (
3.3.10
) and using that
v\equiv u
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}^{+}(p)
, we obtain Estimate (
3.3.9
) and thereby the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.3.5
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(-\infty,0]
,
R>0
,
U_{R}^{-}:=U_{R}^{-}(q)
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R}^{-})
. Then

[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}}^{-}}\leq C(\Lambda,\theta,n,\gamma)\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{R}^{-}}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R}^{-})}\right).

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
\Lambda
,
\theta
,
n
and
\gamma
. Let
p\in U_{R}^{-}(q)
and
\rho>0
such that
U_{\rho}^{-}(p)\subset U_{R}^{-}(q)
. Let
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(U_{\rho}(0),[0,1])
with
\eta\equiv 1
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(0)
that satisfies Estimate (
3.3.4
).
We define
v:=\eta u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(-\infty,0])
by extending with
0
outside of
U_{\rho}^{-}(p)
. Then by Lemma
3.2.3

[D^{4,1}v]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(-\infty,0]}^{(0)}\leq C[Pv]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(-\infty,0]}^{(0)}.

By following the estimates in the proof of Lemma
3.3.3
, we get that for all small
\epsilon>0

[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}^{-}(p)}^{(0)}\leq C(\epsilon)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{-}(p)}^{(0)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}^{-}(p))}\right)+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{-}(p)}^{(0)}.

(3.3.14)

Simons’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
with
A=\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(-\infty,0]
implies the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.3.6
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
,
R>0
,
U_{R+}:=U_{R+}(q)
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R+})
. Then

[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}+}}^{(0)}\leq C(\Lambda,\theta,n,\gamma)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R+}}^{(0)}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R+})}\right).

If
\partial_{S}U_{R+}=\emptyset
, the estimate holds with
[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}=[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}=0
.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
\Lambda
,
\theta
,
n
and
\gamma
. Let
p\in U_{R+}(q)
and
\rho>0
such that
U_{\rho+}(p)\subset U_{R+}(q)
. Let
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(U_{\rho}(p),[0,1])
with
\eta\equiv 1
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(p)
that satisfies Estimate (
3.3.4
).
We define
v:=\eta u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R})
by extending with zero outside of
U_{\rho+}(p)
. By Lemma
3.2.4

\hskip-2.84544pt[D^{4,1}v]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}\leq C\left([Pv]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}+[B_{1}v]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}+[B_{2}v]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}\right).

(3.3.15)

Following the derivation of Estimate (
3.3.8
), we get that for all small
\epsilon>0

[Pv]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}}^{(0)}\leq C(\epsilon)\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p))}\right)+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p)}^{(0)}.

(3.3.16)

To estimate
[B_{1}v]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}
, we note the formulas

\displaystyle[f]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=[\nabla^{3}f]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{space}}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}[\nabla^{k}f]_{\frac{3+\gamma-k}{4},\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{time}},

(3.3.17)

\displaystyle\nabla^{k}(B_{1}v)

\displaystyle=\eta\nabla^{k}(B_{1}u)+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq k+1\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=k+1\end{subarray}}c(\alpha,\beta,a^{ij})\nabla_{\beta}\eta\nabla_{\alpha}u.

(3.3.18)

We insert Equation (
3.3.18
) into Equation (
3.3.17
). Using the analogue of Estimate (
3.3.7
) for the various Hölder seminorms in Equation (
3.3.17
) and Estimate (
3.3.4
), we estimate

\displaystyle[a^{ni}\partial_{i}v]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg([\nabla^{3}(a^{ni}\partial_{i}u)]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p)}^{\operatorname{space}}+\rho^{-\gamma}\|\nabla^{4}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p))}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq 4\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=4\end{subarray}}\rho^{-|\beta|-\gamma}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p))}+\rho^{-|\beta|}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p)}^{\operatorname{space}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{k=0}^{3}[\nabla^{k}(a^{ni}\partial_{i}u)]_{\frac{3+\gamma-k}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p)}^{\operatorname{time}}+\rho^{-3-\gamma+k}\|\nabla^{k+1}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p))}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq k+1\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=k+1\end{subarray}}\rho^{-|\beta|-3-\gamma+k}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p))}+\rho^{-|\beta|}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\frac{3+\gamma-k}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p)}^{\operatorname{time}}\bigg).

Using the definition of the
[\cdot]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}
seminorm and collecting terms, we obtain

\displaystyle[a^{ni}\partial_{i}v]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg([a^{ni}\partial_{i}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p)}^{(3)}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}\rho^{-4-\gamma+k}\|\nabla^{k}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p))}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq 4\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=4\end{subarray}}\rho^{-|\beta|}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p)}^{\operatorname{space}}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq k+1\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=k+1\end{subarray}}\rho^{-|\beta|}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\frac{3+\gamma-k}{4},U_{\rho+}(p)}^{\operatorname{time}}\bigg).

We insert the interpolation estimates from Theorem
1.2.3
and Lemma
1.2.5
. This shows that for all small
\epsilon

\displaystyle[a^{ni}\partial_{i}v]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}\leq

\displaystyle C(\epsilon)\left([a^{ni}\partial_{i}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p)}^{(3)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p))}\right)

\displaystyle+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p)}^{(0)}.

(3.3.19)

To estimate
[B_{2}v]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}
, we essentially repeat the same estimates. First, we note the formulas

\displaystyle[f]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=[\nabla f]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{space}}+\sum_{k=0}^{1}[\nabla^{k}f]_{\frac{1+\gamma-k}{4},\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{time}},

(3.3.20)

\displaystyle\nabla^{k}(B_{2}v)

\displaystyle=\eta\nabla^{k}(B_{2}u)+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k+2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta|\leq k+3\\
|\alpha|+|\beta|=k+3\end{subarray}}c(\alpha,\beta,a^{ij})\nabla_{\beta}\eta\nabla_{\alpha}u.

(3.3.21)

We insert Equation (
3.3.21
) into Equation (
3.3.20
) and use the analog of Estimate (
3.3.7
) for the various Hölder seminorms in Equation (
3.3.20
) and Estimate (
3.3.4
). Afterwards, we insert the interpolation estimates from Theorem
1.2.3
and Lemma
1.2.5
and eventually obtain that for all small
\epsilon

\displaystyle[a^{ni}a^{kl}\partial_{ikl}v]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times\mathbb{R}}\leq

\displaystyle C(\epsilon)\left([a^{ni}a^{kl}\partial_{ikl}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p))}\right)

\displaystyle+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p)}^{(0)}.

(3.3.22)

Inserting Estimates (
3.3.16
), (
3.3.19
) and (
3.3.22
) into Estimate (
3.3.15
) and using that
v\equiv u
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}+}(p)
we get that for all small
\epsilon

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}+}(p)}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle C(\epsilon)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p)}^{(0)}+[B_{1}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p)}^{(3)}+[B_{2}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p)}^{(1)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p))}\right)

\displaystyle+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p)}^{(0)}.

Using the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
3.3.3
, the lemma follows by Simon’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
with
A=\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.7
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0]
,
R>0
,
U_{R+}^{-}:=U_{R+}^{-}(q)
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R+}^{-})
. Then

[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}+}^{-}}^{(0)}\leq C(\Lambda,\theta,n,\gamma)\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{R+}^{-}}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R+}^{-})}\right).

If
\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}=\emptyset
, the estimate holds with
[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}=[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}=0
.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
\Lambda
,
\theta
,
n
and
\gamma
. Let
p\in U_{R+}^{-}(q)
and
\rho>0
such that
U_{\rho+}^{-}(p)\subset U_{R+}^{-}(q)
. Let
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(U_{\rho}(p),[0,1])
with
\eta\equiv 1
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(p)
that satisfies Estimate (
3.3.4
).
We define
v:=\eta u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0])
by extending with
0
outside of
U_{\rho+}^{-}(p)
. Then by Lemma
3.2.6

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}v]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0]}^{(0)}\leq C\bigg(

\displaystyle[Pv]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0]}^{(0)}+[B_{1}v]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times(-\infty,0]}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+[B_{2}v]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times(-\infty,0]}\bigg).

(3.3.23)

By following the estimates from Lemma
3.3.3
, the estimates that lead to estimate (
3.3.19
) and the estimates that lead to Estimate (
3.3.22
), we deduce that for small
\epsilon>0

\displaystyle\hskip-14.51074pt[Pv]^{(0)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0]}\leq C(\epsilon)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p)}^{(0)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}^{-}(p))}\right)+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p)},

(3.3.24)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.51074pt[B_{1}v]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times(-\infty,0]}\leq C(\epsilon)\left([B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}^{-}(p))}\right)+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p)},

(3.3.25)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.51074pt[B_{2}v]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times(-\infty,0]}\leq C(\epsilon)\left([B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}^{-}(p))}\right)+C\epsilon[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p)}.

(3.3.26)

The lemma follows by inserting these Estimates (
3.3.24
), (
3.3.25
) and (
3.3.26
) into Estimate (
3.3.23
), using
v\equiv u
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}+}^{-}(p)
and applying Simon’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
with
A=\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0]
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.8
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)
,
R>0
,
U_{R+}^{+}:=U_{R+}^{+}(q)
,
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R+}^{+})
and
u_{0}:=u(\cdot,0)
. Then

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}+}}\leq C(\Lambda,\theta,n,\gamma)

\displaystyle\bigg([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R+}^{+}(0)}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R+}^{+}}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R+}^{+})}\bigg).

If
\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}=\emptyset
or
\partial_{F}U_{R+}^{+}=\emptyset
, the estimate holds with
[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}=[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}=0
or
[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R+}^{+}}=0
respectively.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
\Lambda
,
\theta
,
n
and
\gamma
. Let
p\in U_{R+}^{+}(q)
and
\rho>0
such that
U_{\rho+}^{+}(p)\subset U_{R+}^{+}(q)
. Let
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(U_{\rho}(p),[0,1])
with
\eta\equiv 1
on
U_{\frac{\rho}{2}+}^{+}(p)
that satisfies Estimate (
3.3.4
). We define
v:=\eta u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty))
by extending with zero outside of
U_{\rho+}^{+}(p)
. By Lemma
3.2.5

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}v]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg([Pv]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)}^{(0)}+[B_{1}v]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times[0,\infty)}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+[B_{2}v]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0\times[0,\infty)}+[\nabla^{4}v(\cdot,0)]_{\gamma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)}\bigg).

(3.3.27)

As in the proof of Lemma
3.3.7
, we may deduce the analog of Estimates (
3.3.24
), (
3.3.25
) and (
3.3.26
). Additionally, following the estimates that lead to Estimate (
3.3.13
), we may derive the analog estimate on
U_{\rho+}^{+}(p)
.
Inserting these into Estimate (
3.3.27
), the lemma follows by Simon’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
with
A=\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)
.
∎

3.3.2
Localized Estimates with Variable Coefficients

Throughout this subsection let
a^{ij}\in C^{0,0,\gamma}(\Omega_{R}(q))
, where depending on the situation on
\Omega_{R}(q)=U_{R}(q)
,
U_{R}^{\pm}(q)
,
U_{R+}^{\pm}(q)
for suitable
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
and
R>0
. We assume that for some
\theta>0

a^{ij}(x,t)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\geq\theta|\xi|^{2}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{for all $(x,t)\in\Omega_{R}(q)$ and $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$}.

For a multiindex
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
of length
|\alpha|=4
, we define
A^{\alpha}
by
\sum_{|\alpha|=4}A^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}=a^{ij}a^{kl}\partial_{ijkl}
. Additionally, for
|\alpha|\leq 3
let
A^{\alpha}\in C^{0,0,\gamma}(\Omega_{R}(q))
be given functions. We assume that for some
M>0
and all
|\alpha|\leq 4

\|A^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R}(q))}\leq MR^{-4+|\alpha|}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636pt[A^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,\Omega_{R}(q)}^{(0)}\leq MR^{-4-\gamma+|\alpha|}.

(3.3.28)

In the situations, where we
\Omega_{\rho}(q)
has a spatial boundary – that is for
U_{R+}(q)
and
U_{R+}^{\pm}(q)
, we also require the following: For a multiindex
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
of length
|\alpha|=3
, we define
b^{\alpha}
by
\sum_{|\alpha|=3}b^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}:=a^{ni}a^{kl}\partial_{ikl}
and assume that for
|\alpha|\leq 2
, we are given functions
b^{\alpha}\in C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q))
. We further assume that for all
|\alpha|\leq 3
,
k\in\set{0,1}
and
k\leq l\leq 1

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}b^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q))}\leq MR^{-3-k+|\alpha|},\hskip 14.22636pt[\nabla^{k}b^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q)}^{(0)}\leq MR^{-3-k-\gamma+|\alpha|},

(3.3.29)

\displaystyle\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636pt[\nabla^{k}b^{\alpha}]_{\frac{1-l+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q)}^{\operatorname{time}}\leq MR^{-4-\gamma-k+l+|\alpha|}.

(3.3.30)

Similarly, for a multiindex
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
of length
|\alpha|=1
, we define
c^{\alpha}
by
\sum_{|\alpha|=1}c^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}:=a^{ni}\partial_{i}
and assume that for
|\alpha|<1
(that is
|\alpha|=0
), we are given functions (a function)
c^{\alpha}\in C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q))
. We further assume that for
|\alpha|\leq 1
,
k\in\set{0,1,2,3}
and
k\leq l\leq 3

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}c^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q))}\leq MR^{-1-k+|\alpha|},\hskip 14.22636pt[\nabla^{k}c^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q)}^{(0)}\leq MR^{-1-k-\gamma+|\alpha|},

(3.3.31)

\displaystyle\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636pt[\nabla^{k}c^{\alpha}]_{\frac{3-l+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q)}^{\operatorname{time}}\leq MR^{-4-\gamma-k+l+|\alpha|}.

(3.3.32)

Finally, we define the operators

P:=\partial_{t}+A^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha},\hskip 14.22636ptB_{1}:=c^{\beta}\nabla_{\beta}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636ptB_{2}:=b^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}.

Remark 3.3.9
.

Assumptions (
3.3.28
)-(
3.3.32
) should rather be seen as the definition of
M
. Indeed, since we assume
A^{\alpha}\in C^{0,0,\gamma}(\Omega_{R}(q))
,
b^{\alpha}\in C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q))
and
c^{\alpha}\in C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}\Omega_{R}(q))
, all left hand sides in Assumptions (
3.3.28
)-(
3.3.32
) are finite and hence (
3.3.28
)-(
3.3.32
) are always satisfied for some
M
.

Lemma 3.3.10
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
,
R>0
,
U_{R}:=U_{R}(q)
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R})
. There exists a constant
C=C(M,\gamma,n,\theta)
such that

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R}}^{(0)}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R})}\right),

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{\frac{R}{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq C(R)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R}}^{(0)}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R})}\right).

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
M
,
\gamma
,
n
and
\theta
. Let
\nu\in(0,1]
,
p_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in U_{R}(q)
and
\rho\in(0,\nu R)
such that
U_{\rho}(p_{0})\subset U_{R}(q)
. We define the operator
P_{0}:=\partial_{t}+\sum_{|\alpha|=4}A^{\alpha}(p_{0})\nabla_{\alpha}
.
Using Lemma
3.3.3
, we have

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(p_{0})}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left([P_{0}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}+[(P-P_{0})u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right).

(3.3.33)

We derive a suitable estimate for
[(P-P_{0})u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}
. To do so, we note

(P-P_{0})u=\sum_{|\alpha|=4}(A^{\alpha}-A^{\alpha}(p_{0}))\nabla_{\alpha}u+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}A^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}u.

(3.3.34)

For a multiindex
\alpha
of length
|\alpha|\leq 3
, we use Estimate (
3.3.7
) and Assumption (
3.3.28
) to estimate

\displaystyle[A^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|A^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+[A^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(R^{-4+|\alpha|}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+R^{-4-\gamma+|\alpha|}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right).

(3.3.35)

Similarly, if
|\alpha|=4
, we estimate

\displaystyle[(A^{\alpha}-A^{\alpha}(p_{0}))\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|A^{\alpha}-A^{\alpha}(p_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+[A^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left([A^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,U_{R}}^{(0)}\rho^{\gamma}[\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+MR^{-\gamma}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(R^{-\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+R^{-\gamma}\|\nabla_{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right).

(3.3.36)

Considering Equation (
3.3.34
), we use Estimates (
3.3.35
) and (
3.3.36
) and the interpolations estimates from Theorem
1.2.3
, to deduce

\displaystyle[(P-P_{0})u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg[MR^{-\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+\sum_{|\alpha|=4}MR^{-\gamma}\rho^{-4}\left(\epsilon\rho^{4+\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+C(\epsilon)\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}MR^{-4+|\alpha|}\rho^{-|\alpha|-\gamma}\left(\epsilon\rho^{4+\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+C(\epsilon)\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}MR^{-4-\gamma+|\alpha|}\rho^{-|\alpha|}\left(\epsilon\rho^{4+\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+C(\epsilon)\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right)\bigg].

To estimate the first term, we use
\rho\leq\nu R
to estimate
R^{-\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}\leq\nu^{\gamma}
. In all other terms, we estimate the terms
R^{-\kappa}
with
\kappa\geq 0
by
R^{-\kappa}\leq\rho^{-\kappa}
. Hence

[(P-P_{0})u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\leq CM(\epsilon+\nu^{\gamma})[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+C(\epsilon)\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}.

(3.3.37)

Inserting this estimate into Estimate (
3.3.33
) we get that for all small
\epsilon
and
\nu

\displaystyle\rho^{4+\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle C(\epsilon,\nu)\left(\rho^{4+\gamma}[Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle+CM(\epsilon+\nu^{\gamma})\rho^{4+\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{U_{\rho}(p_{0})}.

(3.3.38)

Estimate (
3.3.38
) is analogue to Estimate (
3.3.8
). Choosing
\epsilon
and
\nu
small enough, we may apply Simon’s Absorption Lemma
1
1

1

This is possible since the parameter
\delta
from Simon’s Absorption Lemma does not depend on
\nu
.
and deduce the first estimate claimed in the lemma.
The second estimate is a direct consequence of the interpolation estimates in Theorem
1.2.3

∎

Lemma 3.3.11
.

Let
R>0
,
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,\infty)
,
U_{R}^{+}:=U_{R}^{+}(q)
,
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R}^{+})
and
u_{0}:=u(\cdot,0)
. There exists a constant
C=C(M,\gamma,n,\theta)
such that

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R}^{+}}^{(0)}+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R}^{+}}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R}^{+})}\right),

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{\frac{R}{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq C(R)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R}^{+}}^{(0)}+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R}^{+}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R}^{+})}\right).

If
\partial_{F}U_{R}^{+}(q)=\emptyset
, the estimate holds with
[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R}^{+}(q)}=0
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
M
,
\gamma
,
n
and
\theta
. Let
\nu\in(0,1]

p_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in U_{R}^{+}(q)
and
\rho\in(0,\nu R)
such that
U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})\subset U_{R}^{+}(q)
. We establish that for small
\epsilon>0

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}^{+}(p_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle C(\epsilon,\nu)\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})}+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle+CM(\epsilon+\nu^{\gamma})[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}.

(3.3.39)

To prove Estimate (
3.3.39
), we essentially follow the proof of Lemma
3.3.10
.
We define the operator
P_{0}:=\partial_{t}+\sum_{|\alpha|=4}A^{\alpha}(p_{0})\nabla_{\alpha}
.
Using Lemma
3.3.4
, we have

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}^{+}(p_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left([P_{0}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})}+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-68.28644pt\leq C\bigg([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})}+[(P-P_{0})u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})}+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0}))}\bigg).

(3.3.40)

Following the estimates that lead to Estimate (
3.3.37
), we can derive an analogue estimate
[(P-P_{0})u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{+}(p_{0})}
and deduce Estimate (
3.3.39
). The first estimate claimed in the lemma follows from Simon’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
by the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
3.3.10
. The second estimate is a direct consequence of the interpolation estimates in Theorem
1.2.3

∎

Lemma 3.3.12
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(-\infty,0]
,
R>0
,
U_{R}^{-}:=U_{R}^{-}(q)
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R}^{-})
. There exists a constant
C=C(M,\gamma,n,\theta)
such that

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}}^{-}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R}^{-}}^{(0)}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R}^{-})}\right),

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{\frac{R}{2}}^{-})}

\displaystyle\leq C(R)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{R}^{-}}^{(0)}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R}^{-})}\right).

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
M
,
\gamma
,
n
and
\theta
. The proof follows the proof of Lemma
3.3.10
.
Let
\nu\in(0,1]
,
p_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in U_{R}^{-}(q)
and
\rho\in(0,\nu R)
such that
U_{\rho}^{-}(p_{0})\subset U_{R}^{-}(q)
. We define the operator
P_{0}:=\partial_{t}+\sum_{|\alpha|=4}A^{\alpha}(p_{0})\nabla_{\alpha}
.
Using Lemma
3.3.5
, we deduce

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}^{-}(p_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left([P_{0}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{-}(p_{0})}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}^{-}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{-}(p_{0})}+[(P-P_{0})u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{-}(p_{0})}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{-}_{\rho}(p_{0}))}\right).

Following the estimates the lead to Estimate (
3.3.37
), we can derive an analogue estimate for
[(P-P_{0})u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{-}(p_{0})}
and get

[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}}^{-}(p_{0})}\leq C(\epsilon,\nu)\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{-}(p_{0})}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho}^{-}(p_{0}))}\right)+CM(\epsilon+\nu^{\gamma})[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho}^{-}(p_{0})}.

As in the proof of Lemma
3.3.10
, the first estimate claimed in the lemma follows from Simon’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
. The second estimate is a direct consequence of the interpolation estimates in Theorem
1.2.3

∎

Lemma 3.3.13
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
,
R>0
,
U_{R+}:=U_{R+}(q)
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R+})
. There exists a constant
C=C(M,\gamma,n,\theta)
such that

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}+}}\leq

\displaystyle C\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{R+}}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R+})}\right),

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{\frac{R}{2}+})}\leq

\displaystyle C(R)\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{R+}}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R+})}\right).

If
\partial_{S}U_{R+}=\emptyset
, the estimate holds with
[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}=[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}}=0
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
M
,
\gamma
,
n
and
\theta
. We follow the proof of Lemma
3.3.10
. Let
\nu\in(0,1]
,
p_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in U_{R+}(q)
and
\rho\in(0,\nu R)
such that
U_{\rho+}(p_{0})\subset U_{R+}(q)
. We put
A^{\alpha}_{0}:=A^{\alpha}(p_{0})
,
b^{\alpha}_{0}:=b^{\alpha}(p_{0})
and
c^{\alpha}_{0}:=c^{\alpha}(p_{0})
and consider the constant coefficient operators
P_{0}:=\sum_{|\alpha|=4}A^{\alpha}_{0}\nabla_{\alpha}
,
B_{1,0}:=\sum_{|\alpha|=1}c_{0}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}
and
B_{2,0}:=\sum_{|\alpha|=3}b_{0}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}
. By Lemma
3.3.6
we get

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}+}(p_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle C\left([P_{0}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+[B_{1,0}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(3)}+[B_{2,0}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(1)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-68.28644pt\leq C\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+[B_{1}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(3)}+[B_{2}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(1)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-62.59596pt+C\left([(P-P_{0})u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+[(B_{1}-B_{1,0})u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(3)}+[(B_{2}-B_{2,0})u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(1)}\right).

(3.3.41)

We can use the analysis leading to Estimate (
3.3.37
) to estimate
[(P-P_{0})u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}
. Next, we estimate
[(B_{1}-B_{1,0})u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(3)}
. To do so, we note that for
1\leq N\leq 3

\displaystyle\nabla^{N}\left[(B_{1}-B_{1,0})u\right]=

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|=1}(c^{\alpha}-c_{0}^{\alpha})\nabla^{N}\nabla_{\alpha}u+\sum_{|\alpha|<1}c^{\alpha}\nabla^{N}\nabla_{\alpha}u

(3.3.42)

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 1}\sum_{|\beta|\leq N-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\kappa|\leq N\\
|\beta|+|\kappa|=N\end{subarray}}c(\alpha,\beta,\kappa)\nabla_{\kappa}c^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha+\beta}u.

First, we consider
[\nabla^{3}((B_{1}-B_{1,0})u)]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}
. We estimate the leading term from Equation (
3.3.42
). To do so, let
\alpha
be a multi-index of length
|\alpha|=1
. We estimate
\|c^{\alpha}-c^{\alpha}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\leq[c^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}^{\operatorname{space}}\rho^{\gamma}
. Additionally, we use Assumption (
3.3.31
),
\rho\leq\nu R
and an analogue of Estimate (
3.3.7
), to estimate

\displaystyle[(c^{\alpha}-c_{0}^{\alpha})\nabla^{3}\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|c^{\alpha}-c_{0}^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}[\nabla^{4}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}+[c^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}\|\nabla^{4}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left([c^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\rho^{\gamma}[\nabla^{4}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}+[c^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}\|\nabla^{4}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(R^{-\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}[\nabla^{4}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}+R^{-\gamma}\|\nabla^{4}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(\nu^{\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+R^{-\gamma}\|\nabla^{4}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right).

(3.3.43)

Next, for a multiindex
\alpha
of length
|\alpha|<1
(that is
|\alpha|=0)
, we use Assumption (
3.3.31
) and an analogue of Estimate (
3.3.7
) to estimate

\displaystyle[c^{\alpha}\nabla^{3}\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|c^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}[\nabla^{3+|\alpha|}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}+[c^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}\|\nabla^{3+|\alpha|}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(R^{-1+|\alpha|}[\nabla^{3+|\alpha|}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}+R^{-1+|\alpha|-\gamma}\|\nabla^{3+|\alpha|}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right).

(3.3.44)

Next, we estimate the remaining terms in Equation (
3.3.42
) (
N=3
). Let
|\alpha|\leq 1
,
|\beta|\leq 3-1
and
|\kappa|=3-|\beta|
. Using Assumption (
3.3.31
), we estimate

\displaystyle[\nabla_{\kappa}c^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha+\beta}u]^{\operatorname{space}}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|\nabla_{\kappa}c^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}[\nabla_{\alpha+\beta}u]^{\operatorname{space}}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}+[\nabla_{\kappa}c^{\alpha}]^{\operatorname{space}}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}\|\nabla_{\alpha+\beta}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(R^{-1-|\kappa|+|\alpha|}[\nabla^{|\alpha|+|\beta|}u]^{\operatorname{space}}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}+R^{-1-|\kappa|-\gamma+|\alpha|}\|\nabla^{|\alpha|+|\beta|}u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right).

(3.3.45)

Considering the terms
R^{(...)}
appearing in Estimates (
3.3.43
), (
3.3.44
) and (
3.3.45
) , we see that
(...)\leq 0
in all cases. Hence
R^{(...)}\leq\rho^{(...)}
. Inserting the interpolation estimates from Theorem
1.2.3
as we did in the derivation of Estimate (
3.3.37
), we deduce

[\nabla^{3}\big((B_{1}-B_{1,0})u\big)]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{space}}\leq CM(\nu^{\gamma}+\epsilon)[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+C(\epsilon)\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}.

(3.3.46)

For
0\leq k\leq 3
, we consider
[\nabla^{k}((B_{1}-B_{1,0})u)]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}
. We first estimate the leading term from Equation (
3.3.42
). Let
|\alpha|=1
. Using Assumptions (
3.3.31
) and (
3.3.32
),
\rho\leq\nu R
and an analogue of Estimate (
3.3.7
), we estimate

\displaystyle[(c^{\alpha}-c_{0}^{\alpha})\nabla^{k}\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|c^{\alpha}-c_{0}^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}[\nabla^{k+1}u]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}+[c^{\alpha}]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}\|\nabla^{k+1}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left([c^{\alpha}]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\rho^{\gamma}[\nabla^{k+1}u]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}+[c^{\alpha}]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}\|\nabla^{k+1}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(R^{-\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}[\nabla^{k+1}u]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}+R^{-3+k-\gamma}\|\nabla^{k+1}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(\nu^{\gamma}[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+R^{-3+k-\gamma}\|\nabla^{k+1}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right).

(3.3.47)

Next, for
|\alpha|\leq 1
,
|\beta|\leq k-1
and
|\kappa|=k-|\beta|
, we use Assumptions (
3.3.31
) and (
3.3.32
), to estimate

\displaystyle[\nabla_{\kappa}c^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha+\beta}u]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{3+\gamma-k}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|\nabla_{\kappa}c^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}[\nabla_{\alpha+\beta}u]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{3+\gamma-k}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}+[\nabla_{\kappa}c^{\alpha}]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{3+\gamma-k}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}\|\nabla_{\alpha+\beta}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(R^{-1-|\kappa|+|\alpha|}[\nabla^{|\alpha|+|\beta|}u]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{3+\gamma-k}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}+R^{-4-\gamma-|\kappa|+|\alpha|+k}\|\nabla^{|\alpha|+|\beta|}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\bigg(R^{-1-k+|\alpha|+|\beta|}[\nabla^{|\alpha|+|\beta|}u]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{3+\gamma-k}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+R^{-4-\gamma+|\alpha|+|\beta|}\|\nabla^{|\alpha|+|\beta|}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\bigg).

(3.3.48)

Using the same arguments; for
|\alpha|<1
(that is
|\alpha|=0)
, we estimate

\displaystyle[c^{\alpha}\nabla^{k}\nabla_{\alpha}u]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|c^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}[\nabla^{k}u]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}+[c^{\alpha}]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}\|\nabla^{k}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CM\left(R^{-1}[\nabla^{k}u]_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{\operatorname{time}}+R^{-4+k-\gamma}\|\nabla^{k}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right).

(3.3.49)

Considering the terms
R^{(...)}
appearing in Estimates (
3.3.47
), (
3.3.48
) and (
3.3.49
), we see that
(...)\leq 0
in all cases. Hence
R^{(...)}\leq\rho^{(...)}
. Inserting the interpolation estimates from Theorem
1.2.3
and Lemma
1.2.5
, we deduce

\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{k=0}^{3}[\nabla^{k}\left((B_{1}-B_{1,0})u\right)]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{3-k+\gamma}{4},\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}\leq\hskip-2.84544ptCM(\nu^{\gamma}+\epsilon)[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+C(\epsilon)\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}.

(3.3.50)

Combining Estimates (
3.3.46
) and (
3.3.50
), we obtain

[(B_{1}-B_{1,0})u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(3)}\leq CM(\nu^{\gamma}+\epsilon)[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+C(\epsilon)\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}.

(3.3.51)

Following the exact same arguments, the following estimate can be established by using Assumptions (
3.3.29
) and (
3.3.30
):

[(B_{2}-B_{2,0})u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(1)}\leq CM(\nu^{\gamma}+\epsilon)[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+C(\epsilon)\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}.

(3.3.52)

Inserting the analogue of Estimate (
3.3.37
) and Estimates (
3.3.51
) and (
3.3.52
) into Estimate (
3.3.41
), we have shown that for any
p_{0}\in U_{R+}(q)
and
\rho\leq\nu R
such that
U_{\rho+}(p_{0})\subset U_{R+}(q)
, we have

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle C\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+[B_{1}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(3)}+[B_{2}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}^{(1)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle+CM\left(\epsilon+\nu^{\gamma}\right)[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}(p_{0})}+C(\epsilon)\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}(p_{0}))}.

The first estimate in the lemma follows from Simon’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
. The second estimate is a direct consequence of the interpolation estimates in Theorem
1.2.3

∎

Lemma 3.3.14
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times(-\infty,0]
,
R>0
,
U_{R+}^{-}:=U_{R+}^{-}(q)
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R+}^{-})
.
There exists a constant
C=C(M,\gamma,n,\theta)
such that

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}+}^{-}}^{(0)}\leq

\displaystyle C\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{R+}^{-}}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R+}^{-})}\right),

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{\frac{R}{2}+}^{-})}\leq

\displaystyle C(R)\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{R+}^{-}}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R+}^{-})}\right).

If
\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}=\emptyset
, the estimate holds with
[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}=[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{-}}=0
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we will suppress the dependence of various constants
C
on
M
,
\gamma
,
n
and
\theta
. Let
\nu\in(0,1]
,
p_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in U_{R+}^{-}(q)
and
\rho\in(0,\nu R)
such that
U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})\subset U_{R+}^{-}(q)
. We put
A^{\alpha}_{0}:=A^{\alpha}(p_{0})
,
b^{\alpha}_{0}:=b^{\alpha}(p_{0})
and
c^{\alpha}_{0}:=c^{\alpha}(p_{0})
. Additionally, we put
P_{0}:=\sum_{|\alpha|=4}A^{\alpha}_{0}\nabla_{\alpha}
,
B_{1,0}:=\sum_{|\alpha|=1}c_{0}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}
and
B_{2,0}:=\sum_{|\alpha|=3}b_{0}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}
. By Lemma
3.3.7
we get

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}+}^{-}(p_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle C\left([P_{0}u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+[B_{1,0}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(3)}+[B_{2,0}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(1)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-68.28644pt\leq C\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+[B_{1}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(3)}+[B_{2}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(1)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-62.59596pt+C\left([(P-P_{0})u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(0)}\hskip-5.69046pt+[(B_{1}-B_{1,0})u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(3)}\hskip-5.69046pt+[(B_{2}-B_{2,0})u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(1)}\right).

(3.3.53)

Following the arguments that lead to Estimates (
3.3.37
),
(
3.3.51
) and
(
3.3.52
), we obtain suitable estimates for

[(P-P_{0})u]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(0)}
,

[(B_{1}-B_{1,0})u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(3)}
and

[(B_{2}-B_{2,0})u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(1)}
.
Inserting these into Estimate (
3.3.53
), we get

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{\rho}{2}+}^{-}(p_{0})}\leq

\displaystyle C(\epsilon)\left([Pu]_{\gamma,U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(0)}+[B_{1}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(3)}+[B_{2}u]_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}^{(1)}+\rho^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+CM\left(\epsilon+\nu^{\gamma}\right)[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{U_{\rho+}^{-}(p_{0})}.

The first estimate claimed in the lemma follows from Simon’s Absorption Lemma
3.3.2
. The second estimate is a direct consequence of the interpolation estimates in Theorem
1.2.3

∎

Following the proof of Lemma
3.3.14
and swapping Lemma
3.3.7
for Lemma
3.3.8
, the following lemma can be established.

Lemma 3.3.15
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)
,
R>0
,
U_{R+}^{+}:=U_{R+}^{+}(q)
,
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{R+}^{+})
and
u_{0}:=u(\cdot,0)
. There exists a constant
C=C(M,\gamma,n,\theta)
such that

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{\frac{R}{2}+}^{+}}\leq

\displaystyle C\left([Pu]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{R+}^{+}}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R+}^{+}(0)}+R^{-4-\gamma}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R+}^{+})}\right),

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{\frac{R}{2}+}^{+})}\leq

\displaystyle C(R)\left([Pu]_{U_{R+}^{+}}^{(0)}+[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}+[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}+[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R+}^{+}(0)}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{R+}^{+})}\right).

If
\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}=\emptyset
or
\partial_{F}U_{R+}^{+}=\emptyset
, the estimate holds with
[B_{1}u]^{(3)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}=[B_{2}u]^{(1)}_{\gamma,\partial_{S}U_{R+}^{+}}=0
or
[\nabla^{4}u_{0}]_{\gamma,\partial_{F}U_{R+}^{+}}=0
respectively.

3.4
Covering Arguments

In this section, we provide suitable covering arguments that allow us to extend the results from Section
3.3
to a more general setting.

Lemma 3.4.1
.

Let
V\subset\subset V^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be open and bounded sets,
T_{0}>0
and
T\geq T_{0}
. There exists
\rho>0
,
x_{1},...,x_{N}\in V
and
0=t_{0}<t_{1}<...<t_{k_{0}-1}<t_{k_{0}}=T
such that the sets

U_{jk,r}:=U_{r}(x_{j},t_{k})\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,T])\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{where $1\leq j\leq N$, $0\leq k\leq k_{0}$ and $r>0$}

have the following properties:

(1)

For all
1\leq j\leq N
and
0\leq k\leq k_{0}
we have
U_{jk,\rho}\subset U_{jk,2\rho}\subset V^{\prime}\times[0,T]
.

(2)

Each
U_{jk,2\rho}
is of one of the following forms:
U_{2\rho}(x_{j},t_{k})
,
U^{+}_{2\rho}(x_{j},t_{k})
or
U_{2\rho}^{-}(x_{j},t_{k})
.

(3)

V\times[0,T]\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}\bigcup_{k=0}^{k_{0}}U_{jk,\rho}
.

The parameters
N
and
\rho
depend only on the sets
V
,
V^{\prime}
and the parameter
T_{0}
but not on
T
. Moreover, for a function
\varphi\in C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])
we have

\displaystyle[\varphi]^{\operatorname{space}}_{\gamma,V}\leq N\max_{j,k}[\varphi]^{\operatorname{space}}_{\gamma,U_{jk,\rho}}+C(\rho,\gamma)\max_{j,k}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{jk,\rho})},

\displaystyle[\varphi]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{\gamma}{4},V}\leq 4\max_{j,k}[\varphi]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{\gamma}{4},U_{jk,\rho}}+C(\rho,\gamma)\max_{j,k}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{jk,\rho})}.

Proof.

Let
\rho\in(0,1)
be a fixed number such that
1000\rho^{4}<T_{0}
and such that for all
x\in\bar{V}
we have
B_{2\rho}(x)\subset V^{\prime}
.
By compactness we can select finitely many points
x_{j}\in V
with
1\leq j\leq N(V,V^{\prime},\rho(V,V^{\prime},T_{0}))
such that
V\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}B_{\rho}(x_{j})
.

Definition of the Covering

Let
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
be the unique natural number that satisfies
k_{0}\rho^{4}\leq T<(k_{0}+1)\rho^{4}
. By definition of
\rho
, we have
k_{0}\geq 1000
. We define
t_{k_{0}}:=T
and
t_{k}:=k\rho^{4}
for all
0\leq k\leq k_{0}-1
. Clearly
0=t_{0}<t_{1}<...<t_{k_{0}}=T
. For
1\leq k\leq k_{0}-1
, we define

\displaystyle I_{0}

\displaystyle:=(t_{0}-\rho^{4},t_{0}+\rho^{4})\cap[0,T]=[0,\rho^{4}),

\displaystyle I_{k_{0}}

\displaystyle:=(t_{k_{0}}-\rho^{4},t_{k_{0}}+\rho^{4})\cap[0,T]=(T-\rho^{4},T],

\displaystyle I_{k}

\displaystyle:=(t_{k}-\rho^{4},t_{k}+\rho^{4})\cap[0,T]=((k-1)\rho^{4},(k+1)\rho^{4}).

By definition of
k_{0}
, we have
[0,T]=I_{0}\cup...\cup I_{k_{0}}
.
For
1\leq j\leq N
,
0\leq k\leq k_{0}
and
r=\rho,2\rho
we define

\displaystyle U_{jk,r}

\displaystyle:=U_{r}(x_{j},t_{k})\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,T])=B_{r}(x_{j})\times(t_{k}-r^{4},t_{k}+r^{4}).

By construction, the sets
U_{jk,\rho}
cover
V\times[0,T]
and
U_{jk,2\rho}\subset V^{\prime}\times[0,T]
. Finally, since
k_{0}\geq 1000
,
U_{jk,2\rho}
cannot have a nonempty intersection with
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times 0
and
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times T
. So, sets
U_{jk,r}
satisfy the claimed properties.

Spatial Hölder Norm

Let
x\neq y\in V
and
t\in[0,T]
. Let
0\leq i\leq k_{0}
such that
t\in I_{i}
. Further, we assume without loss of generality that
x\in B_{\rho}(x_{1})
. If
y\not\in B_{2\rho}(x_{1})
, then
|x-y|\geq\rho
and hence

\frac{|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t)|}{|x-y|^{\gamma}}\leq\frac{1}{\rho^{\gamma}}(|\varphi(x,t)|+|\varphi(y,t)|)\leq\frac{2}{\rho^{\gamma}}\max_{j,k}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{jk,\rho})}.

(3.4.1)

If
y\in B_{2\rho}(x_{1})
, we use Lemma
1.1.3
Indeed
B_{2\rho}(x_{1})
is convex and covered by the convex sets
B_{2\rho}(x_{1})\cap B_{\rho}(x_{j})
with
1\leq j\leq N
. So, applying Lemma
1.1.3
to the function
\varphi(\cdot,t)
gives

\frac{|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(y,t)|}{|x-y|^{\gamma}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}[\varphi(\cdot,t)]_{\gamma,B_{\rho}(x_{j})}\leq N\max_{j,k}[\varphi(\cdot,t)]_{\gamma,B_{\rho}(x_{j})}\leq N\max_{j,k}[\varphi]_{\gamma,U_{jk,\rho}}^{\operatorname{space}}.

(3.4.2)

Combining Estimates (
3.4.1
) and (
3.4.2
), we obtain the claimed estimate.

Temporal seminorm

Let
x\in V
and
0\leq t\neq s\leq T
. Without loss of generality we assume that
x\in B_{\rho}(x_{1})
and
t<s
. First, we assume that
|t-s|\geq\rho^{4}
. Then

\frac{|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(x,s)|}{|t-s|^{\frac{\gamma}{4}}}\leq\frac{1}{\rho^{\gamma}}(|\varphi(x,t)|+|\varphi(x,s)|)\leq\frac{2}{\rho^{\gamma}}\max_{j,k}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{jk,\rho})}.

(3.4.3)

In the last step, we used that the sets
U_{jk,\rho}
cover
V\times[0,T]
.
Now we consider the case
|t-s|<\rho^{4}
. Let
0\leq k\leq k_{0}
be the largest
k
such that
t\in I_{k}
and
l\geq k
be the smallest
l\geq k
such that
s\in I_{l}
. Note that the convex set
\bigcup_{a=k}^{l}I_{a}
is covered by the convex sets
I_{a}
with
k\leq a\leq l
. So, applying Lemma
1.1.3
to the function
\varphi(x,\cdot)
, we obtain

\frac{|\varphi(x,t)-\varphi(x,s)|}{|t-s|^{\frac{\gamma}{4}}}\leq[\varphi(x,\cdot)]_{\frac{\gamma}{4},\bigcup_{a=k}^{l}I_{a}}\leq\sum_{a=k}^{l}[\varphi(x,\cdot)]_{\frac{\gamma}{4},I_{a}}\leq(l-k+1)\max_{j,k}[\varphi]_{\frac{\gamma}{4},U_{jk,\rho}}^{\operatorname{time}}.

(3.4.4)

If
k\leq k_{0}-4
, then
t\in I_{k}
implies
s\in I_{k}\cup I_{k+1}\cup I_{k+2}
, since
\sup I_{k}=(k+1)\rho^{4}
and
\inf I_{k+3}=(k+2)\rho^{4}
. So
l-k+1\leq 3
. If
k\geq k_{0}-3
, then
l\in\set{k_{0}-3,k_{0}-2,k_{0}-1,k_{0}}
and so
l-k+1\leq 4
.
Combining Estimates (
3.4.3
), (
3.4.4
) and
l-k+1\leq 4
we deduce the claimed estimate.
∎

Remark 3.4.2

(On the Lower Bound
T_{0}
)
.

In the proof of Lemma
3.4.1
, we have used the lower bound
T_{0}
to ensure that
k_{0}\geq 1000
. This is because we need to avoid the situation where a parabolic ball has nonempty intersection with
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times 0
and
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times T
.

We can repeat the same arguments to obtain a good covering for domains in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)
.

Lemma 3.4.3
.

Let
V\subset\subset V^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)
be open and bounded sets such that
V\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0)\neq\emptyset
,
T_{0}>0
and
T\geq T_{0}
. There exists
\rho>0
,
x_{1},...,x_{N}
and
0=t_{0}<t_{1}<...<t_{k_{0}}=T
such that the sets

U_{jk,r}:=U_{r}(x_{j},t_{k})\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)\times[0,T])\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{where $1\leq j\leq N$, $0\leq k\leq k_{0}$ and $r>0$}

have the following properties:

(1)

For all
1\leq j\leq N
and
0\leq k\leq k_{0}
we have
U_{jk,\rho}\subset U_{jk,2\rho}\subset V^{\prime}\times[0,T]
.

(2)

Each
U_{jk,2\rho}
is of one of the following forms:
U_{2\rho}(x_{j},t_{k})
,
U^{+}_{2\rho}(x_{j},t_{k})
,
U_{2\rho}^{-}(x_{j},t_{k})
,
U_{2\rho+}(x_{j},t_{k})
,
U^{+}_{2\rho+}(x_{j},t_{k})
or
U_{2\rho+}^{-}(x_{j},t_{k})
.

(3)

V\times[0,T]\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}\bigcup_{k=0}^{k_{0}}U_{jk,\rho}
.

The parameters
N
and
\rho
depend only on the sets
V
and
V^{\prime}
and the parameter
T_{0}
but not on
T
. Moreover, for a function
\varphi\in C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])
we have

\displaystyle[\varphi]^{\operatorname{space}}_{\gamma,V}\leq N\max_{j,k}[\varphi]^{\operatorname{space}}_{\gamma,U_{jk,\rho}}+C(\rho,\gamma)\max_{j,k}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{jk,\rho})},

\displaystyle[\varphi]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{\gamma}{4},V}\leq 4\max_{j,k}[\varphi]^{\operatorname{time}}_{\frac{\gamma}{4},U_{jk,\rho}}+C(\rho,\gamma)\max_{j,k}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{jk,\rho})}.

Using Lemmas
3.4.1
and
3.4.3
, we can now extend the estimated from Section
3.3
to general domains.

Corollary 3.4.4
.

Let
V\subset\subset V^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be open and bounded sets,
T_{0}>0
,
T\geq T_{0}
and
\Lambda,\theta>0
. There exists a constant
C=C(V,V^{\prime},T_{0},\gamma,\Lambda,\theta)
such that for all

P:=\partial_{t}+a^{ij}a^{kl}\partial_{ijkl}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}A^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}

with coefficients
a^{ij},A^{\alpha}\in C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])
satisfying

\displaystyle a^{ij}(x,t)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\geq\theta|\xi|^{2}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{for all $(x,t)\in V^{\prime}\times[0,T]$ and $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$},

(3.4.5)

\displaystyle\|a^{ij}\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])},\ \|A^{\alpha}\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}\leq\Lambda,

(3.4.6)

and all
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])

\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(V\times[0,T])}\leq C\left(\|Pu\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}+\|u(\cdot,0)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(V^{\prime})}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}\right).

The important observation in Lemma
3.4.4
lies in the fact that the constant
C(V,V^{\prime},T_{0},\gamma,\Lambda,\theta)
does not depend on
T
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we suppress the dependence of constants on
V
,
V^{\prime}
,
T_{0}
,
\gamma
,
\Lambda
and
\theta
. We choose
\rho>0
and
U_{jk,r}
as in Lemma
3.4.1
. Each
U_{jk,2\rho}
is of the form
U_{2\rho}(q)
,
U_{2\rho}^{+}(q)
or
U_{2\rho}^{-}(q)
for some
q
. Depending on which is the case we can use Lemmas
3.3.10
,
3.3.11
or
3.3.12
to get the following estimates for all
1\leq j\leq N
and
0\leq k\leq k_{0}
:

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(U_{jk,\rho})}\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|Pu\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(U_{jk,2\rho})}+\|u(\cdot,0)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\partial_{F}U_{jk,2\rho})}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{jk,2\rho})}\right)

(3.4.7)

Here we have condensed notation by putting
\|u(\cdot,0)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\partial_{F}U_{jk,2\rho})}:=0
, if
\partial_{F}U_{jk,2\rho}=\emptyset
. First, as
U_{jk,\rho}
cover
V\times[0,T]
and
U_{jk,2\rho}\subset V^{\prime}\times[0,T]
we have the estimate

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1}(V\times[0,T])}\leq

\displaystyle\max_{j,k}\|u\|_{C^{4,1}(U_{jk,\rho})}

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\leq C(\rho)\left(\|Pu\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}+\|u(\cdot,0)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(V^{\prime})}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}\right).

(3.4.8)

To estimate the Hölder seminorms, we use the estimates for the spatial and temporal seminorms from Lemma
3.4.1
. Inserting Estimate (
3.4.7
), we deduce

\displaystyle[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,V\times[0,T]}

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho)\left(\max_{j,k}[D^{4,1}u]^{(0)}_{\gamma,U_{jk,\rho}}+\max_{j,k}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{jk,\rho})}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\leq C(\rho)\left(\|Pu\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}+\|u(\cdot,0)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(V^{\prime})}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}\right).

(3.4.9)

The corollary follows by combining Estimates (
3.4.8
) and (
3.4.9
) and recalling that
\rho
from Lemma
3.4.1
only depends on
V
,
V^{\prime}
and
T_{0}
.
∎

We establish the analogue fact for
V\subset\subset V^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)

Corollary 3.4.5
.

Let
V\subset\subset V^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)
be open and bounded with
V\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0)\neq\emptyset
,
T_{0}>0
,
T\geq T_{0}
and
\Lambda,\theta>0
. We put
\partial_{S}V:=V\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0)
,
\partial_{S}V^{\prime}:=V^{\prime}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0)
. There exists a constant
C=C(V,V^{\prime},T_{0},\gamma,\Lambda,\theta)
such that for all

\displaystyle P:=\partial_{t}+a^{ij}a^{kl}\partial_{ijkl}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}A^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}

\displaystyle B_{1}:=a^{ni}\partial_{i}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 0}c^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636ptB_{2}:=a^{ni}a^{kl}\partial_{ikl}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}b^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}

with coefficients
a^{ij},A^{\alpha}\in C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])
,
a^{ij},c^{\alpha}\in C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V^{\prime}\times[0,T])
and
b^{\alpha}\in C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V^{\prime}\times[0,T])
satisfying

\displaystyle a^{ij}(x,t)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\geq\theta|\xi|^{2}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{for all $(x,t)\in V^{\prime}\times[0,T]$ and $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$},

(3.4.10)

\displaystyle\|a^{ij}\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])},\ \|A^{\alpha}\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}\leq\Lambda,

(3.4.11)

\displaystyle\|a^{ij}\|_{C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V^{\prime}\times[0,T])},\ \|c^{\alpha}\|_{C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V^{\prime}\times[0,T])},\ \|b^{\alpha}\|_{C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}\leq\Lambda,

(3.4.12)

and all
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(V\times[0,T])}\leq C

\displaystyle\left(\|Pu\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}+\|u(\cdot,0)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(V^{\prime})}+\|B_{1}u\|_{C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\|B_{2}u\|_{C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(V^{\prime}\times[0,T])}\right).

The important observation in Lemma
3.4.5
lies in the fact that the constant
C(V,V^{\prime},T_{0},\gamma,\Lambda,\theta)
does not depend on
T
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as the one for Corollary
3.4.4
. Instead of Lemma
3.4.1
we use the covering provided by Lemma
3.4.3
and then use either Lemma

3.3.10
,

3.3.11
,

3.3.12
,

3.3.13
,

3.3.14

or

3.3.15

depending on whether
U_{kj,2\rho}
is of the form
U_{2\rho}(q)
,
U_{2\rho}^{+}(q)
,
U_{2\rho}^{-}(q)
,
U_{2\rho+}(q)
,
U_{2\rho+}^{+}(q)
or
U_{2\rho+}^{-}(q)
.
∎

4
|
Schauder Theory on Manifolds

Throughout this chapter, let
M
be a smooth, compact, connected
n
-dimensional manifold with boundary
\partial M\neq\emptyset
.

4.1
Schauder Estimates on Manifolds

We first establish the definition of elliptic and parabolic operators and suitable boundary conditions on
M
. To do so, we fix a good atlas
\mathcal{A}=\set{(\varphi_{\nu},U_{\nu})\ }{\ 1\leq\nu\leq m}
on
M
. Additionally, we put
V_{\nu}:=\varphi_{\nu}(U_{\nu})
. The following definitions make reference to this choice. However, following arguments similar to the ones in the proofs of Lemmas
1.3.3
and
1.3.5
, it is easy to see that these definitions are independent of the choice of good atlas.

Definition 4.1.1

(2-Elliptic Operators on
M
)
.

Let
L:C^{4}(M)\rightarrow C^{0}(M)
be a linear, differential operator. We say that
L
is a
2-elliptic operator of class
C^{0,\gamma}(M)
, if for all
u\in C^{4}(M)

(Lu)\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}=a_{\varphi_{\nu}}^{ij}a_{\varphi_{\nu}}^{kl}\partial_{ijkl}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1})+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}A_{\varphi_{\nu}}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1})\vskip-5.69046pt

(4.1.1)

with coefficients
a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}},A^{\alpha}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\in C^{0,\gamma}(V_{\nu})
and strictly elliptic coefficients
a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}}
.

In the following, we require a measure for the
quality
of 2-elliptic operators. For that purpose, we define

\displaystyle\|L\|_{C^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(M)}:=\max_{1\leq\nu\leq m}\left[\max_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\|A^{\alpha}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{0,\gamma}(V_{\nu})}+\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\|a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{0,\gamma}(V_{\nu})}\right],

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathcal{A}}(L):=\sup\{\theta>0\ |\ a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}}(x)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\geq\theta|\xi|^{2}\textrm{ for all }1\leq\nu\leq m,\ x\in V_{\nu},\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\}.

Following arguments similar to the proof of Lemma
1.3.3
, it is easy to see that for two good atlases
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
there is a constant
C=C(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\gamma)>0
such that

\|L\|_{C^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{B}}(M)}\leq C\|L\|_{C^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(M)}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Theta_{\mathcal{B}}(L)\geq\frac{1}{C}\Theta_{\mathcal{A}}(L).

(4.1.2)

While e.g. stating
\Theta(L)\geq 1
does not make sense without making reference to a concrete good atlas
\mathcal{A}
, it is a meaningful thing to write
\Theta(L_{s})\geq\theta_{0}>0
for a family of operators
L_{s}
with
s\in[0,1]
without having to resort to a good atlas. However, it has to be understood that the concrete value of
\theta_{0}
depends on the choice of good atlas.

Next, we introduce
L
-compatible boundary conditions. To motivate this definition, we consider a model problem. Assuming we are given a Riemannian metric
g
on
M
, we can consider the operator
L=\Delta_{g}^{2}
. Denoting the exterior unit normal along
\partial M
by
\nu
and the Laplace-Beltrami operator by
\Delta_{g}
, we can define natural boundary operators by

B_{1}u:=\frac{\partial u}{\partial\nu}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636ptB_{2}u:=\frac{\partial\Delta_{g}u}{\partial\nu}.

In local coordinates, they are given by the formulas

B_{1}u=-\left(g^{nn}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g^{ni}\partial_{i}u\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{and}\hskip 14.22636ptB_{2}u=-\left(g^{nn}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}g^{ni}g^{kl}\partial_{ikl}u+\textrm{`lower order terms'}.

(4.1.3)

Finally, we recall that for a domain
V\subset\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[0,\infty)
we put
\partial_{S}V:=V\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times 0)
.

Definition 4.1.2

(
L
-compatible Boundary Conditions)
.

Let
L
be a 2-elliptic operator of class
C^{0,\gamma}(M)
. We say that a linear operator
B=(B_{1},B_{2}):C^{4}(M)\rightarrow C^{3}(\partial M)\times C^{1}(\partial M)
defines
L
-compatible boundary conditions of class

C^{3,\gamma}(\partial M)\times C^{1,\gamma}(\partial M)
, if for all
u\in C^{4}(M)

\displaystyle(B_{1}u)\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(a^{nn}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a^{ni}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\partial_{i}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1})+c_{\varphi_{\nu}}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}),

\displaystyle(B_{2}u)\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(a^{nn}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a^{ni}a^{kl}\partial_{ikl}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1})+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}b_{\varphi_{\nu}}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}),

with
a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}}
as in Equation (
4.1.1
) and
a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}},c_{\varphi_{\nu}}\in C^{3,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu})
and
b^{\alpha}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\in C^{1,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu})
.

We introduce a measure for the quality of such an operator
B
by defining

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{C^{3,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial M)}:=\max_{1\leq\nu\leq m}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\left(a^{nn}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a^{ni}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{3,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu})}+\|c_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{3,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu})}\right],

\displaystyle\|B_{2}\|_{C^{1,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial M)}:=\max_{1\leq\nu\leq m}\left[\max_{1\leq i,k,l\leq n}\|\left(a^{nn}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a^{ni}_{\varphi_{\nu}}a^{kl}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{1,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu})}+\max_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\|b^{\alpha}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{1,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu})}\right].

Additionally, we note that the analog of the first estimate in Estimate (
4.1.2
) is true.

Given a
2
-elliptic operator
L
, we can consider the associated parabolic operator
P:=\partial_{t}+L
. However, we also want to allow for parabolic operators with time-dependent coefficients.

Definition 4.1.3

(2-Parabolic Operators on
M\times[0,T]
)
.

Let
T>0
and
P:C^{4,1}(M\times[0,T])\rightarrow C^{0,0}(M\times[0,T])
be a linear, differential operator. We say that
P
is a
2-parabolic operator of class

C^{0,0,\gamma}(M\times[0,T])
, if for all
u\in C^{4,1}(M)

(Pu)\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}=\partial_{t}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1})+a_{\varphi_{\nu}}^{ij}a_{\varphi_{\nu}}^{kl}\partial_{ijkl}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1})+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 3}A_{\varphi_{\nu}}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1})

(4.1.4)

with coefficients
a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}},A^{\alpha}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\in C^{0,0,\gamma}(V_{\nu}\times[0,T])
and strictly elliptic
a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}}
.

As in the elliptic case, we introduce a measure for the quality of a 2-parabolic operator.

\displaystyle\|P\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(M\times[0,T])}:=\max_{1\leq\nu\leq m}\left[\max_{|\alpha|\leq 3}\|A^{\alpha}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V_{\nu}\times[0,T])}+\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\|a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(V_{\nu}\times[0,T])}\right],

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathcal{A}}(P):=\sup\{\theta>0\ |\ a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}}(x,t)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\geq\theta|\xi|^{2}\textrm{ for all }1\leq\nu\leq m,\ (x,t)\in V_{\nu}\times[0,T],\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\}.

Additionally, we note that the analogs of the estimates in Estimate (
4.1.2
) are true. As in the elliptic case, we now introduce
P
-compatible boundary conditions.

Definition 4.1.4

(
P
-compatible Boundary Conditions)
.

Let
T>0
and
P
be a 2-parabolic operator. We say that a linear operator
B=(B_{1},B_{2}):C^{4,1}(M\times[0,T])\rightarrow C^{3,0}(\partial M\times[0,T])\times C^{1,0}(\partial M\times[0,T])
defines
P
-compatible boundary conditions of class
C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial M\times[0,T])\times C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial M\times[0,T])
, if for all
u\in C^{4,1}(M\times[0,T])

\displaystyle(B_{1}u)\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(a^{nn}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a^{ni}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\partial_{i}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1})+c_{\varphi_{\nu}}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}),

\displaystyle(B_{2}u)\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(a^{nn}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a^{ni}_{\varphi_{\nu}}a^{kl}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\partial_{ikl}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1})+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 2}b_{\varphi_{\nu}}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\alpha}(u\circ\varphi_{\nu}^{-1}),

with
a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}}
as in Equation (
4.1.4
) and
a^{ij}_{\varphi_{\nu}},c_{\varphi_{\nu}}\in C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu}\times[0,T])
,
b^{\alpha}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\in C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu}\times[0,T])
.

Again, we introduce a measure for the quality of such a boundary operator
B
by defining

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{C^{3,0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial M\times[0,T])}:=\max_{1\leq\nu\leq m}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\left(a^{nn}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a^{ni}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu}\times[0,T])}+\|c_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu}\times[0,T])}\right],

\displaystyle\|B_{2}\|_{C^{1,0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial M\times[0,T])}:=\max_{1\leq\nu\leq m}\left[\max_{1\leq i,k,l\leq n}\|\left(a^{nn}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}a^{ni}_{\varphi_{\nu}}a^{kl}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu}\times[0,T])}+\max_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\|b^{\alpha}_{\varphi_{\nu}}\|_{C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial_{S}V_{\nu}\times[0,T])}\right].

Additionally, we note that the analog of the first estimate in Estimate (
4.1.2
) is true.

To proceed, we require Ehrling’s Lemma. For a proof, we refer to
[
16
]
, Chapter 7, Section 2, Theorem 7.30.

Proposition 4.1.5

(Ehrling’s Lemma)
.

Let
X
,
Y
and
Z
be Banach spaces such that
X\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow Y
compactly and
Y\hookrightarrow Z
continuously. Then for all
\epsilon>0
there exists a constant
C(\epsilon)>0
such that for all
x\in X

\|x\|_{Y}\leq\epsilon\|x\|_{X}+C(\epsilon)\|x\|_{Z}.

Using Corollaries
3.4.4
and
3.4.5
, we can now deduce Schauder estimates on
M
. The following estimate contains an
L^{2}
-norm. Since we have not equipped
M
with a Riemannian metric, we stress that this norm should be understood in the sense of Definition
1.4.3
and not the
L^{2}
-norm induced by a Riemannian metric.

Theorem 4.1.6
.

Let
T_{0}>0
,
T\geq T_{0}
,
\mathcal{A}
be a good atlas on
M
,
P
be a 2-parabolic operator of class
C^{0,0,\gamma}(M\times[0,T])
and let
B
define
P
-compatible boundary conditions of class
C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial M\times[0,T])\times C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial M\times[0,T])
. Then, for any
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(M\times[0,T])

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}(M\times[0,T])}\leq
