T\geq 1
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])
be the solution to Problem (
4.5.2
) with
f=0
and
h_{1}=h_{2}=0
. Then for all
t\in[0,T]

\|u(t)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\leq C\left(e^{-12t}\|u_{0}^{\perp}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}+\|u_{0}^{\parallel}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\right).

where
C=C_{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+},\gamma)
depends on
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}
and
\gamma
but importantly not on
T
.

Proof.

We put
u^{\parallel}(t):=(u(t))^{\parallel}
and
u^{\perp}(t):=(u(t))^{\perp}
. Using the PDE and the boundary conditions from Problem (
4.5.2
) and partial integration, we compute

\displaystyle\dot{u}^{\parallel}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta(\Delta+2)u^{\parallel}=

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\dot{u}(t),1\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}{\langle 1,1\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}1+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{\langle\dot{u}(t),\omega_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}{\langle\omega_{i},\omega_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}\omega_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\langle\Delta(\Delta+2)u(t),1\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}{\langle 1,1\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}1-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{\langle\Delta(\Delta+2)u(t),\omega_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}{\langle\omega_{i},\omega_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}\omega_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\langle u(t),\Delta(\Delta+2)1\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}{\langle 1,1\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}1-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{\langle u(t),\Delta(\Delta+2)\omega_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}{\langle\omega_{i},\omega_{i}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}}\omega_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Since
u^{\parallel}(t)\in\operatorname{span}(\phi_{0},\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
we have
\Delta(\Delta+2)u^{\parallel}(t)=0
and hence
\dot{u}^{\parallel}=0
. So
u^{\parallel}(t)=u^{\parallel}(0)=u_{0}^{\parallel}
and thus

\|u^{\parallel}(t)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}=\|u_{0}^{\parallel}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}.

(4.5.3)

Next, we consider the evolution of
u^{\perp}=u-u^{\parallel}
. Using
\dot{u}^{\parallel}=\Delta(\Delta+2)u^{\parallel}=0
we obtain

\displaystyle\dot{u}^{\perp}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta(\Delta+2)u^{\perp}=\dot{u}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta(\Delta+2)u=0.

Using the boundary conditions from Problem (
4.5.2
), it is clear that
\partial_{\nu}u^{\perp}=\partial_{\nu}\Delta u^{\perp}=0
. Also
u^{\perp}(0)=u_{0}^{\perp}
. We define
v(t):=e^{12t}u^{\perp}(t)
. It follows that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{v}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta(\Delta+2)v=12v&\displaystyle\textrm{in $\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T]$},\\
\displaystyle\frac{\partial v}{\partial\nu}=\frac{\partial\Delta v}{\partial\nu}=0&\displaystyle\textrm{along $\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T]$},\\
\displaystyle v(0)=u_{0}^{\perp}&\displaystyle\textrm{on $\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}$}.\end{array}\right.

(4.5.4)

For
l=0,1,2
, the functions
\Delta^{l}v(t)
are continuous and, by Theorem
2.3.5
, they can therefore be expanded into Neumann eigenfunctions. We claim

\Delta^{l}v(\cdot,t)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}(t)(-\lambda_{k})^{l}\phi_{k}\hskip 14.22636pt\textrm{in $L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})$}.

Indeed by the boundary conditions
\partial_{\nu}v=\partial_{\nu}\Delta v=0
from Problem (
4.5.4
) we have
\langle\phi_{k},\Delta^{2}v(t)\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}=-\lambda_{k}\langle\phi_{k},\Delta v\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}=\lambda_{k}^{2}\langle v,\phi_{k}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}
.
By definition,
v\perp\phi_{0},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})
and hence
c_{0}=c_{1}=c_{2}=0
. Additionally,
\lambda_{k}(\lambda_{k}-2)\geq 24
for
k\geq 3
since
\lambda_{k}\geq 6
for such
k
. Consequently

-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}}v(t)\Delta(\Delta+2)v(t)d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}(2-\lambda_{k})|c_{k}(t)|^{2}\leq-12\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}|c_{k}(t)|^{2}=-12\|v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}^{2}.

Using this estimate, we compute

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}}v(t)^{2}d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}}v(t)\dot{v}(t)d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}}-v(t)\frac{1}{2}\Delta(\Delta+2)v(t)+12v(t)^{2}d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\leq 0.

This implies
\|v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\leq\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}=\|u_{0}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Applying the estimate from Theorem
4.1.6
we deduce that for all
t\in[0,T]

\|v(t)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\leq\|v\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}\leq C(\|u_{0}^{\perp}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})})\leq C\|u_{0}^{\perp}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}.

Recalling that
v(t)=e^{12t}u(t)
we obtain

\|u^{\perp}(t)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\leq Ce^{-12t}\|u_{0}^{\perp}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}.

(4.5.5)

Combining Estimates (
4.5.3
) and (
4.5.5
), we deduce the theorem.
∎

Corollary 4.5.4
.

Let
T\geq 1
and
u\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])
be the solution to Problem (
4.5.2
). Then for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\left(e^{-12t}\|u_{0}^{\perp}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}+\|u_{0}^{\parallel}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\right)

\displaystyle+C_{2}\left(\|f\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}+\|h_{1}\|_{C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}+\|h_{2}\|_{C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}\right)

where
C_{1}=C_{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+},\gamma)
depends on
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}
and
\gamma
but importantly not on
T
and
C_{2}=C_{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+},\gamma,T)
depends on
\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}

\gamma
and
T
.

Proof.

We study the elliptic problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\Delta^{2}w_{0}=0&\displaystyle\textrm{in $\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}$,}\\
\displaystyle\frac{\partial w_{0}}{\partial\nu}=h_{1}(\cdot,0),\ \frac{\partial\Delta w_{0}}{\partial\nu}=h_{2}(\cdot,0)&\displaystyle\textrm{along $\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}$,}\\
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}}w_{0}d\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=0.&\end{array}\right.

(4.5.6)

By Corollary
4.4.5
, Problem (
4.5.6
) has a unique solution
w_{0}\in C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})
. By Corollary
4.4.2
, it satisfies the Schauder estimate

\|w_{0}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\leq C\left(\|h_{1}(\cdot,0)\|_{C^{3,\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}+\|h_{2}(\cdot,0)\|_{C^{1,\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\right).

(4.5.7)

We consider the parabolic problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{w}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta(\Delta+2)w=f&\displaystyle\textrm{in $\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T]$},\\
\displaystyle\frac{\partial w}{\partial\nu}=h_{1},\ \ \frac{\partial\Delta w}{\partial\nu}=h_{2}&\displaystyle\textrm{along $\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T]$},\\
\displaystyle w(0)=w_{0}&\displaystyle\textrm{on $\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}$}.\end{array}\right.

(4.5.8)

By construction of
w_{0}
, the necessary compatibility conditions are satisfied. So, by Theorem
4.3.11
, Problem (
4.5.8
) has a unique solution
w\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])
. Combining the Schauder estimate provided by Corollary
4.2.9
with Estimate (
4.5.7
), we deduce that
w
satisfies the Schauder estimate

\displaystyle\|w\|_{C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}\leq C_{2}

\displaystyle\bigg(\|f\|_{C^{0,0,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}+\|h_{1}\|_{C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}

(4.5.9)

\displaystyle+\|h_{2}\|_{C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}\bigg),

where
C_{2}=C_{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+},\gamma,T)
. Next, we put
v_{0}:=u_{0}-w_{0}
and consider the parabolic equation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\dot{v}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta(\Delta+2)v=0&\displaystyle\textrm{in $\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T]$},\\
\displaystyle\frac{\partial v}{\partial\nu}=0,\ \ \frac{\partial\Delta v}{\partial\nu}=0&\displaystyle\textrm{along $\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T]$},\\
\displaystyle v(0)=v_{0}&\displaystyle\textrm{on $\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}$}.\end{array}\right.

(4.5.10)

By construction of
v_{0}
, the necessary compatibility conditions are satisfied. So, by Theorem
4.3.11
, Problem (
4.5.10
) has a unique solution
v\in C^{4,1,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])
, which, by Theorem
4.5.3
, satisfies the decay estimate

\|v(t)\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\leq C_{1}\left(e^{-12t}\|v_{0}^{\perp}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}+\|v_{0}^{\parallel}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}\right),

(4.5.11)

where
C_{1}=C_{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+},\gamma)
but independent of
T
. Let
*\in\set{\perp,\parallel}
. We use Estimate (
4.5.7
) to estimate

\displaystyle\|v_{0}^{*}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{0}^{*}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}+\|w_{0}^{*}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{0}^{*}\|_{C^{4,\gamma}(\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+})}+C\left(\|h_{1}\|_{C^{3,0,\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}+\|h_{2}\|_{C^{1,0,\gamma}(\partial\mathbb{S}^{2}_{+}\times[0,T])}\right).

(4.5.12)

Since
u=v+w
, the corollary follows by combining Estimates (
4.5.9
), (
4.5.11
) and (
4.5.12
).
∎
